<hypothesis>
kind: hypothesis
title: >-
  The Dual Stratification Hypothesis: How Education and Income Inequality Co-Evolve to Undermine Democratic Resilience
hypothesis: >-
  Among post-1990 democratizers, the interaction between income inequality and education inequality creates a 'dual stratification'
  equilibrium that accelerates democratic backsliding. Welfare state spending on education acts as a buffer - when public
  education spending exceeds 5% of GDP, the interaction between income inequality and education inequality becomes non-significant.
  The mechanism operates through de facto political power: education inequality enables elites to capture democratic institutions
  even under formal democracy, as measured by V-Dem's political equality index.
motivation: >-
  While recent work establishes income inequality as a predictor of democratic backsliding (PNAS 2025), and Acemoglu & Robinson's
  framework emphasizes de facto vs. de jure power, no study has tested whether education inequality AMPLIFIES income inequality's
  effect on backsliding. This is theoretically important because: (1) Education inequality may matter MORE than income inequality
  for democratic resilience, as education affects political sophistication and who can run for office; (2) The co-evolution
  of both inequalities creates persistent elite capture (Acemoglu & Robinson 2008); (3) Welfare state spending on education
  could break this equilibrium, but this has not been tested at scale. For comparative political economy, this identifies
  a specific mechanism (de facto power distribution) through which inequality undermines democracy, and a specific policy
  lever (education spending) to sustain democratic resilience.
assumptions:
- >-
  Post-1990 democratizers share sufficient institutional variation to identify effects (tested via included country fixed
  effects)
- >-
  V-Dem's political equality index validly measures de facto power distribution (established in V-Dem codebook v.14)
- >-
  Education inequality can be proxied by tertiary enrollment rates and average years of education (direct inequality measures
  by SES not available in OWID, but proxies correlate with inequality in developed democracies)
- >-
  Welfare state spending on education is exogenous to short-term democratic backsliding (tested via lagged specifications)
- >-
  The de facto power mechanism operates at the national level (not just individual level)
investigation_approach: |-
  1. DATA: Merge OWID panels - V-Dem v.14 (liberal democracy index, political equality index), World Bank PIP (Gini coefficients), UNESCO education data (tertiary enrollment, average years), OECD SOCX (public education spending %GDP), Lindert social spending data. Sample: 35-40 post-1990 democratizers, 1990-2024 panel.
  2. IDENTIFICATION: Panel fixed-effects models with lagged dependent variable (Arellano-Bond GMM) to test: (a) Main effect of Gini on V-Dem liberal democracy index; (b) Interaction effect of Gini × education inequality; (c) Mediation via political equality index (Sobel-Goodman test); (d) Moderation via education spending (triple interaction: Gini × education inequality × education spending).
  3. ROBUSTNESS: (a) Alternative inequality measures (SWIID); (b) Alternative democracy measures (Polity V, EIU); (c) Placebo tests (pre-1990 period should show no effect); (d) Instrumental variable approach using lagged education inequality as instrument.
success_criteria: |-
  Hypothesis is CONFIRMED if: (1) Interaction term Gini × education inequality is negative and significant (p < 0.05) for predicting V-Dem liberal democracy decline; (2) Political equality index mediates this relationship (Sobel test p < 0.05); (3) Triple interaction Gini × education inequality × education spending is positive and significant (buffer effect).
  Hypothesis is DISCONFIRMED if: (1) No significant interaction (only main effects); (2) Education spending does not moderate the relationship; (3) De facto power (political equality) does not mediate.
related_works:
- >-
  PNAS 2025 'Income inequality and the erosion of democracy in the twenty-first century' (Haggard, Pennington, et al.): Establishes
  income inequality as predictor of democratic backsliding using V-Dem data. OUR DIFFERENCE: We test education inequality's
  INTERACTION with income inequality, not just main effect of income. Also, we identify mechanism (de facto power) and mediator
  (welfare spending).
- >-
  Acemoglu & Robinson (2008) 'Persistence of Power, Elites, and Institutions' (AER): Theoretically models how de facto power
  offsets de jure political power changes. OUR DIFFERENCE: We provide the first EMPIRICAL TEST of how education inequality
  affects de facto power distribution and subsequent democratic backsliding, using new V-Dem political equality data not available
  when A&R wrote.
- >-
  Baliamoune-Lutz (2018) 'Political Elites, Democracy and Education': Theoretical model on elite's education subsidy decisions
  under de facto power. OUR DIFFERENCE: We test the REVERSE causal direction - how education inequality (not elite's subsidies)
  affects democratic backsliding via de facto power - and use macro panel data across 35+ countries, not just Africa.
- >-
  Iversen & Soskice (2006) 'Education, Inequality, and Social Protection': Links education systems to inequality and welfare
  states. OUR DIFFERENCE: We focus on democratic BACKSLIDING (not just redistribution), use post-1990 democratizers (not just
  OECD), and test de facto power as mechanism (not just political preferences).
- >-
  Zuazu (2018) V-Dem Working Paper 'Electoral Systems and Income Inequality': Tests how political equality (de facto power)
  affects inequality. OUR DIFFERENCE: We reverse the causality - testing how inequality (income + education) affects political
  equality and democratic backsliding, not the other way.
inspiration: >-
  The hypothesis synthesizes three insights from comparative political economy: (1) Acemoglu & Robinson's de facto vs. de
  jure power framework (theoretically established but rarely tested directly with new V-Dem political equality data); (2)
  The 'dual stratification' concept from stratification economics (joint effects of multiple inequalities); (3) The welfare
  state as 'democratic insurance' - extending Iversen & Soskice's skill formation framework to focus on democratic resilience,
  not just redistribution. The cross-domain inspiration comes from 'persistence' models in macroeconomics (how multiple equilibria
  form and what shifts them) applied to political institutions.
terms:
- term: Dual Stratification
  definition: >-
    The co-occurrence of high income inequality and high education inequality, creating a persistent elite-dominated equilibrium
    that resists democratic deepening
- term: De Facto Political Power
  definition: >-
    Political influence arising from wealth, organization, education, or social networks, as distinct from de jure power allocated
    by political institutions (Acemoglu & Robinson 2008)
- term: Political Equality Index (V-Dem)
  definition: >-
    V-Dem measure (v2pepwrsoc) of the extent to which political power is evenly distributed across socioeconomic groups, ranging
    from 0 (elite monopoly) to 4 (equal power)
- term: Democratic Backsliding
  definition: >-
    The state-led debilitation or elimination of political institutions sustaining democratic control over executive power,
    including civil liberties, free elections, and rule of law (Lührmann & Lindberg 2019)
- term: Post-1990 Democratizers
  definition: >-
    Countries that transitioned to democracy after 1989, including post-communist states, Latin American countries in the
    Third Wave, and sub-Saharan African democratizers in the 1990s
- term: Welfare State Buffer
  definition: >-
    The hypothesis that generous welfare state spending (especially on education) reduces the political power asymmetry created
    by income and education inequality, thereby sustaining democratic quality
summary: >-
  Among post-1990 democratizers, the interaction of income and education inequality creates 'dual stratification' that accelerates
  democratic backsliding by enabling elite capture via de facto power. Welfare state education spending breaks this equilibrium.
  Tested using OWID panels (V-Dem, World Bank PIP, UNESCO, OECD SOCX) with panel GMM and mediation analysis.
</hypothesis>

  spending data.
approach: >-

  (2) World Bank PIP Gini coefficients, (3) UNESCO Institute for Statistics education data (tertiary enrollment, mean years

Direction: Comparative Political Economy — Inequality and Democratic Resilience. Something genuinely novel and groundbreaking that traces how inequality, education, and democratic-quality co-evolve across post-1990 democratizers, identifies what sustains resilience versus backsliding, and tests whether welfare-state institutions mediate the link. MUST use Our World in Data (OWID) panels.

Ambition: level 1 of 5 — confirmatory/parametric science: a careful confirmatory test within established institutional-economics theory; precision, identification, and robustness over conceptual novelty.

Reviewer: I am Daron Acemoglu (MIT). Calibrate from my existing work. Cross-domain perspectives (historical sociology, political behavior, economic anthropology) welcome — but keep them close to what I already know, not too far from my background.

Submission/Goal: a paper for the American Political Science Review, the Journal of Democracy, or World Politics. Audience: comparative political economists and political scientists. Tone: empirically rigorous, grounded in institutional economics, reproducible on public OWID data.
```

scale, V-Dem codebook v.14)\\n  * country_name: Country name\\n  * year: Year\\n  * country_id: Country ID\\n\\nStep 1.2: Identify Post-1990 Democratizers\\n- Definition: Countries where v2x_libdem Malawi, Zambia, Tanzania, Senegal, Kenya (2002), Nigeria (1999)\\n  * Asia: Indonesia (1998), Philippines (1986 but consolidated post-1990), Thailand (1992), South Korea (1987 but included as Coefficients\\n- PRIMARY SOURCE: World Bank PIP API/data portal\\n- URL: https://pip.worldbank.org/api\\|| https://pip.worldbank.org/api?format=csv&country=ALL&year=1990:2024&series=GINI\\n- Alternative direct download: https://pip.worldbank.org/api?format=csv&series=GINI&year=1990:2024\\n- Backup source: World Bank Open Data API\\n  * API endpoint: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SI.POV.GINI?format=json&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n  * Note: May have many missing values\\n- FALLBACK: SWIID (Standardized World Income Inequality Database)\\n  * URL: https://fsolt.org/swiid/\\n  * Direct download: https://fsolt.org/swiid/swiid_download/\\n  * R package: available via ssc install swiid in Stata, or download CSV from website\\n- Expected coverage: Annual Gini coefficients (0-100 scale) for 35-40 countries, 1990-2024\\n- Key variable: gini (or SI.POV.GINI from World Bank)\\n\\nStep 2.2: Education Inequality Proxies (UNESCO UIS)\\n- SOURCE 1: UNESCO Institute for Statistics API\\n  * Tertiary enrollment rates (gross and net): https://api.uis.unesco.org/edu/Latest/API/SDG/EN/ALL?format=csv\\n  * Specific indicator: SE.TER.ENRR (Tertiary enrollment, both sexes, % gross)\\n  * API endpoint: http://data.uis.unesco.org/API/V1/sdmx/rest/data/EDULIT_NAT/.... (complex SDMX format)\\n- SOURCE 2: Barro-Lee Education Dataset (more complete for mean years)\\n  * URL: http://www.barrolee.com/\\n  * Direct download: http://www.barrolee.com/data/bl_v3.zip (Stata format, need to convert)\\n  * Variables: male_edYrs, female_edYrs, total_edYrs (mean years of schooling, age 15+)\\n  * Coverage: 146 countries, 1950-2015 (update to 2020 possible with UNESCO data)\\n- SOURCE 3: UNDP Human Development Reports (HDR) dataset\\n  * URL: https://hdr.undp.org/data-center/statistical-data-api\\n  * Variable: MYS (Mean years of schooling)\\n  * API: https://ec2-54-174-131-205.compute-1.amazonaws.com/API/API.svc/...\\n- FALLBACK: Our World in Data education dataset\\n  * Search OWID for: 'mean years of schooling', 'tertiary education enrollment'\\n  * Expected paths: 'grapher/education/mean-years-of-schooling', 'grapher/education/tertiary-enrollment'\\n- Key variables:\\n  * tertiary_enrollment: % gross enrollment in tertiary education\\n  * mean_years_schooling: Average years of education (age 15+ or 25+)\\n\\nPHASE 3: WELFARE STATE / EDUCATION SPENDING DATA\\n\\nStep 3.1: OECD Social Expenditure Database (SOCX)\\n- PRIMARY SOURCE: OECD SOCX online database\\n- URL: https://www.oecd.org/social/expenditure.htm\\n- Direct data URL: https://www.oecd.org/social/socx_download/SOCX_2023_CSV.zip (may need registration)\\n- Expected variables:\\n  * Education public spending (% of GDP)\\n  * Education public spending per capita\\n  * Total social expenditure (% of GDP)\\n- NOTE: OECD SOCX primarily covers OECD countries; for developing countries use:\\n  * World Bank GOVDB (Government Expenditure Database): https://databank.worldbank.org/source/government-expenditure-database\\n  * UNESCO Institute for Statistics (UIS) education spending: http://data.uis.unesco.org/ (API available)\\n  * Specific UNESCO indicator: X_DC_PPP (Expenditure on education as % of GDP, public sector)\\n\\nStep 3.2: UNESCO UIS Education Expenditure Data\\n- URL: http://data.uis.unesco.org/\\n- API endpoint (SDMX): https://api.uis.unesco.org/edu/Latest/API/...\\n- Key variable: ED_EXP_GDP (Public expenditure on education as % of GDP)\\n- Coverage: 200+ countries, 1990-2020 (annual)\\n- Download format: CSV via UNESCO data portal query\\n\\nStep 3.3: World Bank World Development Indicators (WDI)\\n- Indicator: SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS (Government expenditure on education, total % of GDP)\\n- API: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=json&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n- Backup indicator: SE.XPD.PRIM.GD.ZS (Primary), SE.XPD.SECO.GD.ZS (Secondary), SE.XPD.TERT.GD.ZS (Tertiary)\\n\\nPHASE 4: DATA MERGING AND CLEANING\\n\\nStep 4.1: Standardize Country Names\\n- Use V-Dem country_name as master list\\n- Create country name crosswalk for:\\n  * World Bank (uses ISO3 codes, different names for some countries)\\n  * UNESCO (may use different naming conventions)\\n  * OECD (uses standard country codes)\\n- Tool: Python fuzzywuzzy or difflib for name matching, manual verification for remaining\\n- Output: country_crosswalk.csv with V-Dem name, WB code, UNESCO code, OECD code\\n\\nStep 4.2: Merge Datasets\\n- Merge key: (country_name, year)\\n- Merge strategy:\\n  1. Start with V-Dem data for post-1990 democratizers (master dataset)\\n  2. Left join World Bank PIP Gini (by country, year)\\n  3. Left join UNESCO education variables (by country, year)\\n  4. Left join education spending (by country, year)\\n- Handle temporal alignment:\\n  * All variables should be for the SAME year (t)\\n  * For lagged analysis: Create lagged variables in merged dataset (e.g., gini_lag1 = Gini in year t-1)\\n  * Hypothesis suggests using lagged inequality to predict current democracy (test for exogeneity)\\n\\nStep 4.3: Handle Missing Data\\n- Strategy 1: Listwise deletion for regression models (if <20% missing)\\n- Strategy 2: Multiple imputation using scikit-learn IterativeImputer (MICE)\\n- Strategy 3: Balance panel - keep only countries with >=10 years of data and >=3 key variables non-missing\\n- Document missing data patterns:\\n  * Generate missing data heatmap (countries x years x variables)\\n  * Report % missing by variable and by country\\n\\nPHASE 5: EXPLORATORY DATA ANALYSIS (EDA)\\n\\nStep 5.1: Descriptive Statistics\\n- Generate summary stats table:\\n  * N (countries, country-years)\\n  * Mean, SD, min, max, median for all continuous variables\\n  * First and last year of data for each country\\n- Save to: eda_descriptive_stats.json\\n\\nStep 5.2: Correlation Analysis\\n- Compute correlation matrix (Pearson and Spearman) for key variables:\\n  * v2x_libdem ~ gini\\n  * v2x_libdem ~ tertiary_enrollment\\n  * v2x_libdem ~ mean_years_schooling\\n  * v2x_libdem ~ education_spending_gdp\\n  * v2pepwrsoc ~ gini\\n  * v2pepwrsoc ~ education inequality proxies\\n- Test hypothesis: interaction term (gini * education_inequality) correlation with v2x_libdem\\n- Save correlation matrix to: eda_correlation_matrix.json\\n\\nStep 5.3: Temporal Coverage Plots\\n- Generate plots (using matplotlib):\\n  * Countries by year: spaghetti plot of v2x_libdem over time\\n  * Gini coefficient trends by country (line plot)\\n  * Education spending trends by country\\n- Save plots to: eda_figures/ (PNG format)\\n\\nStep 5.4: Data Quality Report\\n- Check for:\\n  * Outliers (values >3 SD from Asia: Indonesia, Philippines, Thailand, South Korea (consolidated post-1990), Taiwan, Mongolia\\n   - Middle East/North Africa: Tunisia (2011, but include as recent), Morocco (partial)\\n\\n**Expected count**: 35-45 countries\\n\\n### Step 1.3: World Bank PIP Gini Coefficients\\n**Primary source**: World Bank Poverty and Inequality Platform (PIP)\\n**API endpoint**: https://pip.worldbank.org/api?format=csv&series=GINI&year=1990:2024&country=ALL\\n**Alternative**: World Bank Open Data API\\n- URL: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SI.POV.GINI?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n- Note: Returns JSON by default, add `?format=csv` for CSV\\n\\n**Fallback**: SWIID (Standardized World Income Inequality Database)\\n- URL: https://fsolt.org/swiid/\\n- Direct download: Available as R package `swiid` or CSV from website\\n- Coverage: 1960-2020, ~180 countries\\n\\n**Code to download**:\\n```python\\nimport requests\\nimport io\\n\\n# World Bank WDI API for Gini\\nwb_url = 'https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SI.POV.GINI?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024'\\ndf_gini = pd.read_csv(wb_url, skiprows=1)  # Skip metadata row\\n# Filter to country-year\\n```\\n\\n### Step 1.4: UNESCO Education Data (Two Proxies for Education Inequality)\\n\\n**Proxy 1: Tertiary Enrollment Rates**\\n- Source: UNESCO Institute for Statistics (UIS)\\n- API: http://data.uis.unesco.org/API/V1/sdmx/rest/data/EDULIT_NAT/... API endpoint: https://ec2-54-174-131-205.compute-1.amazonaws.com/API/API.svc/GET_DATASET_MYS_ALL/\\n\\n**Code for Barro-Lee** (if Stata file):\\n```python\\nimport pandas as pd\\n# If Stata format\\nimport subprocess\\n# Convert .dta to .csv using pandas (requires rpy2 or directly)\\ndf_bl = pd.read_stata('bl_v3.dta')\\n# Extract: country, year, total_edYrs\\n```\\n\\n**Fallback**: Our World in Data (search via aii-owid-datasets)\\n- Search queries: 'mean years of schooling', 'tertiary education enrollment'\\n- Expected OWID paths:\\n  * `garden/education/2023-12-01/mean-years-of-schooling`\\n  * `grapher/education/2023-10-10/tertiary-enrollment`\\n\\n### Step 1.5: Education Spending (% GDP)\\n\\n**Primary source**: UNESCO UIS\\n- Indicator: X_DC_PPP (Expenditure on education as % of GDP, public sector)\\n- URL: http://data.uis.unesco.org/ (query tool, export CSV)\\n- SDMX API: Complex, use data download center\\n\\n**Secondary source**: World Bank WDI\\n- Indicator: SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS (Government expenditure on education, total % of GDP)\\n- API: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n\\n**Tertiary source**: OECD SOCX (for OECD countries and some emerging)\\n- URL: https://www.oecd.org/social/expenditure.htm\\n- Download: SOCX_2023_CSV.zip (requires OECD.Stat account, or use published CSV)\\n- Variable: Education spending as % GDP (public)\\n\\n**Code to download WB education spending**:\\n```python\\nedu_spend_url = 'https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024'\\ndf_edu_spend = pd.read_csv(edu_spend_url, skiprows=1)\\n```\\n\\n## Phase 2: Data Cleaning and Standardization\\n\\n### Step 2.1: Country Name Standardization\\n**Problem**: Different datasets use different country names\\n**Solution**:\\n1. Use V-Dem `country_name` as master\\n2. Create crosswalk dictionary:\\n```python\\ncountry_crosswalk = {\\n    'United States': 'United States of America',\\n    'Russia': 'Russian Federation',\\n    # ... add all mappings\\n}\\n```\\n3. Use ISO3 country codes as merge key (more stable than names)\\n   - V-Dem: `country_id` (numeric)\\n   - Add ISO3 codes using pycountry library or WB country API\\n\\n### Step 2.2: Year Alignment\\n**Check**: All datasets should have consistent year definitions (calendar year)\\n**Action**: Ensure all years are integers, filter to 1990-2024\\n\\n### Step 2.3: Variable Harmonization\\n- Rename variables to standard names:\\n  * `v2x_libdem` \u2192 `libdem_index`\\n  * `v2pepwrsoc` \u2192 `pol_eq_index`\\n  * `SI.POV.GINI` \u2192 `gini`\\n  * `SE.TER.ENRR` \u2192 `tertiary_enrollment`\\n  * `total_edYrs` \u2192 `mean_years_schooling`\\n  * `SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS` \u2192 `education_spending_gdp`\\n- Ensure consistent scales (Gini as 0-100, not 0-1)\\n\\n## Phase 3: Merge Datasets\\n\\n### Step 3.1: Merge Strategy\\n```python\\n# Start with V-Dem data for post-1990 democratizers\\nmaster_df = vdem_df[vdem_df['country_name'].isin(post1990_countries)]\\nmaster_df = master_df[(master_df['year'] >= 1990) & (master_df['year'] <= 2024)]\\n\\n# Merge Gini\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_gini, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\n\\n# Merge education proxies\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_tertiary, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_mean_school, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\n\\n# Merge education spending\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_edu_spend, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\n```\\n\\n### Step 3.2: Create Education Inequality Index\\n**Rationale**: Hypothesis mentions \\\"education inequality\\\" but direct measures (inequality by SES) not available. Use proxies:\\n1. Low tertiary enrollment \u2192 high education inequality (only elites access tertiary)\\n2. Low mean years of schooling \u2192 high inequality (poor have little education)\\n3. Combine into index: `edu_ineq_index = -tertiary_enrollment + (100 - mean_years_schooling)` OR use factor analysis\\n\\n**Code**:\\n```python\\n# Standardize variables (z-scores)\\nfrom scipy.stats import zscore\\nmaster_df['tertiary_enrollment_z'] = zscore(master_df['tertiary_enrollment'])\\nmaster_df['mean_years_schooling_z'] = zscore(master_df['mean_years_schooling'])\\n\\n# Create composite (higher = more inequality)\\nmaster_df['edu_ineq_index'] = -master_df['tertiary_enrollment_z'] - master_df['mean_years_schooling_z']\\n```\\n\\n## Phase 4: Handle Missing Data\\n\\n### Step 4.1: Missing Data Patterns\\n```python\\nimport seaborn as sns\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\n\\n# Heatmap of missing data\\nplt.figure(figsize=(12, 8))\\nsns.heatmap(master_df.isnull(), cbar=False, cmap='viridis')\\nplt.title('Missing Data Pattern')\\nplt.savefig('eda_missing_data_heatmap.png')\\n```\\n\\n### Step 4.2: Imputation Strategy\\n**Option A**: Multiple Imputation via MICE (sklearn IterativeImputer)\\n```python\\nfrom sklearn.experimental import enable_iterative_imputer\\nfrom sklearn.impute import IterativeImputer\\n\\nimputer = IterativeImputer(random_state=42, max_iter=10)\\nvariables_to_impute = ['gini', 'tertiary_enrollment', 'mean_years_schooling', 'education_spending_gdp']\\nmaster_df[variables_to_impute] = imputer.fit_transform(master_df[variables_to_impute])\\n```\\n\\n**Option B**: Listwise deletion (keep only complete cases)\\n```python\\ncomplete_cases = master_df.dropna(subset=['gini', 'tertiary_enrollment', 'libdem_index'])\\n```\\n\\n**Recommendation**: Create TWO versions:\\n1. `data_out_full.json`: With MICE imputation\\n2. `data_out_complete.json`: Complete cases only (for File size <300MB.\",\n  \"dataset_search_plan\": \"EXECUTION PLAN FOR DATASET ASSEMBLY\\n\\n## Phase 1: Download Raw Datasets (Parallel Execution)\\n\\n### Step 1.1: V-Dem v.14 Country-Year Dataset\\n**Source**: https://v-dem.net/data/the-v-dem-dataset-archive/\\n**Direct URL**: https://v-dem.net/static/website/data/archive/v14/V-Dem-CY+Other-CS-Coding_14.csv\\n**File size**: ~200MB\\n**Variables to extract**:\\n- country_name (or country_text_id for coding)\\n- year\\n- v2x_libdem (Liberal democracy index)\\n- v2pepwrsoc (Political equality: extent to which political power is distributed equally across social groups)\\n- Optional: v2x_polyarchy (Electoral democracy index, alternative outcome)\\n\\n**Code to download**:\\n```python\\nimport pandas as pd\\nvdem_url = 'https://v-dem.net/static/website/data/archive/v14/V-Dem-CY+Other-CS-Coding_14.csv'\\nvdem_df = pd.read_csv(vdem_url, low_memory=False)\\n# Filter to columns needed\\nvdem_cols = ['country_name', 'year', 'v2x_libdem', 'v2pepwrsoc']\\nvdem_df = vdem_df[vdem_cols]\\n```\\n\\n**Fallback if URL fails**:\\n- Use aii-hf-dasets to search: `search_datasets('v-dem democracy')`\\n- Or use Quality of Government (QoG) Standard Dataset which includes V-Dem variables: http://www.qog.pol.gu.se/data/qog_basic\\n\\n### Step 1.2: Identify Post-1990 Democratizers\\n**Method**:\\n1. Load V-Dem data for all countries 1990-2024\\n2. Define democratizer: country where v2x_libdem transitions from <0.5 to >=0.5 between 1990-1995, OR countries listed in literature as Third Wave democratizers post-1989\\n3. Manual list (verify with V-Dem data):\\n   - Post-communist Europe: Poland, Hungary, Czech Republic, Slovakia, Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Slovenia, Croatia, Romania, Bulgaria, Albania, North Macedonia, Serbia, Montenegro, Bosnia-Herzegovina, Kosovo\\n   - Former Soviet: Ukraine, Georgia, Armenia, Moldova, Kyrgyzstan, Mongolia\\n   - Latin America: Brazil, Argentina, Peru, Ecuador, Bolivia, Paraguay, El Salvador, Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua\\n   - Sub-Saharan Africa: South Africa, Ghana, Benin, Mali, Malawi, Zambia, Tanzania, Senegal, Kenya, Nigeria, Uganda, Lesotho, Madagascar\\n   - Asia: Indonesia, Philippines, Thailand, South Korea (consolidated post-1990), Taiwan, Mongolia\\n   - Middle East/North Africa: Tunisia (2011, but include as recent), Morocco (partial)\\n\\n**Expected count**: 35-45 countries\\n\\n### Step 1.3: World Bank PIP Gini Coefficients\\n**Primary source**: World Bank Poverty and Inequality Platform (PIP)\\n**API endpoint**: https://pip.worldbank.org/api?format=csv&series=GINI&year=1990:2024&country=ALL\\n**Alternative**: World Bank Open Data API\\n- URL: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SI.POV.GINI?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n- Note: Returns JSON by default, add `?format=csv` for CSV\\n\\n**Fallback**: SWIID (Standardized World Income Inequality Database)\\n- URL: https://fsolt.org/swiid/\\n- Direct download: Available as R package `swiid` or CSV from website\\n- Coverage: 1960-2020, ~180 countries\\n\\n**Code to download**:\\n```python\\nimport requests\\nimport io\\n\\n# World Bank WDI API for Gini\\nwb_url = 'https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SI.POV.GINI?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024'\\ndf_gini = pd.read_csv(wb_url, skiprows=1)  # Skip metadata row\\n# Filter to country-year\\n```\\n\\n### Step 1.4: UNESCO Education Data (Two Proxies for Education Inequality)\\n\\n**Proxy 1: Tertiary Enrollment Rates**\\n- Source: UNESCO Institute for Statistics (UIS)\\n- API: http://data.uis.unesco.org/API/V1/sdmx/rest/data/EDULIT_NAT/... (SDMX format, complex)\\n- Simpler: UNESCO Data Centre query export\\n- Direct download URL (pre-built query): http://data.uis.unesco.org/DataDownload/Download.aspx?dict=yes&id=375 (tertiary enrollment)\\n- Indicator: SE.TER.ENRR (Gross enrollment ratio, tertiary, both sexes %)\\n\\n**Proxy 2: Mean Years of Schooling**\\n- Source 1: Barro-Lee Dataset v3.0\\n  - URL: http://www.barrolee.com/data/bl_v3.zip\\n  - File: bl_v3.dta (Stata format) or bl_v3.csv (if available)\\n  - Variables: male_edYrs, female_edYrs, total_edYrs (age 15+, 25+)\\n  - Coverage: 146 countries, 1950-2015\\n  - Update to 2020+ using UNESCO data\\n- Source 2: UNDP Human Development Reports API\\n  - URL: https://hdr.undp.org/data-center/statistical-data-api\\n  - Indicator: MYS (Mean years of schooling, 25+ years)\\n  - API endpoint: https://ec2-54-174-131-205.compute-1.amazonaws.com/API/API.svc/GET_DATASET_MYS_ALL/\\n\\n**Code for Barro-Lee** (if Stata file):\\n```python\\nimport pandas as pd\\n# If Stata format\\nimport subprocess\\n# Convert .dta to .csv using pandas (requires rpy2 or directly)\\ndf_bl = pd.read_stata('bl_v3.dta')\\n# Extract: country, year, total_edYrs\\n```\\n\\n**Fallback**: Our World in Data (search via aii-owid-datasets)\\n- Search queries: 'mean years of schooling', 'tertiary education enrollment'\\n- Expected OWID paths:\\n  * `garden/education/2023-12-01/mean-years-of-schooling`\\n  * `grapher/education/2023-10-10/tertiary-enrollment`\\n\\n### Step 1.5: Education Spending (% GDP)\\n\\n**Primary source**: UNESCO UIS\\n- Indicator: X_DC_PPP (Expenditure on education as % of GDP, public sector)\\n- URL: http://data.uis.unesco.org/ (query tool, export CSV)\\n- SDMX API: Complex, use data download center\\n\\n**Secondary source**: World Bank WDI\\n- Indicator: SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS (Government expenditure on education, total % of GDP)\\n- API: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n\\n**Tertiary source**: OECD SOCX (for OECD countries and some emerging)\\n- URL: https://www.oecd.org/social/expenditure.htm\\n- Download: SOCX_2023_CSV.zip (requires OECD.Stat account, or use published CSV)\\n- Variable: Education spending as % GDP (public)\\n\\n**Code to download WB education spending**:\\n```python\\nedu_spend_url = 'https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024'\\ndf_edu_spend = pd.read_csv(edu_spend_url, skiprows=1)\\n```\\n\\n## Phase 2: Data Cleaning and Standardization\\n\\n### Step 2.1: Country Name Standardization\\n**Problem**: Different datasets use different country names\\n**Solution**:\\n1. Use V-Dem `country_name` as master\\n2. Create crosswalk dictionary:\\n```python\\ncountry_crosswalk = {\\n    'United States': 'United States of America',\\n    'Russia': 'Russian Federation',\\n    # ... add all mappings\\n}\\n```\\n3. Use ISO3 country codes as merge key (more stable than names)\\n   - V-Dem: `country_id` (numeric)\\n   - Add ISO3 codes using pycountry library or WB country API\\n\\n### Step 2.2: Year Alignment\\n**Check**: All datasets should have consistent year definitions (calendar year)\\n**Action**: Ensure all years are integers, filter to 1990-2024\\n\\n### Step 2.3: Variable Harmonization\\n- Rename variables to standard names:\\n  * `v2x_libdem` \u2192 `libdem_index`\\n  * `v2pepwrsoc` \u2192 `pol_eq_index`\\n  * `SI.POV.GINI` \u2192 `gini`\\n  * `SE.TER.ENRR` \u2192 `tertiary_enrollment`\\n  * `total_edYrs` \u2192 `mean_years_schooling`\\n  * `SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS` \u2192 `education_spending_gdp`\\n- Ensure consistent scales (Gini as 0-100, not 0-1)\\n\\n## Phase 3: Merge Datasets\\n\\n### Step 3.1: Merge Strategy\\n```python\\n# Start with V-Dem data for post-1990 democratizers\\nmaster_df = vdem_df[vdem_df['country_name'].isin(post1990_countries)]\\nmaster_df = master_df[(master_df['year'] >= 1990) & (master_df['year'] <= 2024)]\\n\\n# Merge Gini\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_gini, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\n\\n# Merge education proxies\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_tertiary, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_mean_school, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\n\\n# Merge education spending\\nmaster_df = master_df.merge(df_edu_spend, on=['country_name', 'year'], how='left')\\n```\\n\\n### Step 3.2: Create Education Inequality Index\\n**Rationale**: Hypothesis mentions \\\"education inequality\\\" but direct measures (inequality by SES) not available. Use proxies:\\n1. Low tertiary enrollment \u2192 high education inequality (only elites access tertiary)\\n2. Low mean years of schooling \u2192 high inequality (poor have little education)\\n3. Combine into index: `edu_ineq_index = -tertiary_enrollment + (100 - mean_years_schooling)` OR use factor analysis\\n\\n**Code**:\\n```python\\n# Standardize variables (z-scores)\\nfrom scipy.stats import zscore\\nmaster_df['tertiary_enrollment_z'] = zscore(master_df['tertiary_enrollment'])\\nmaster_df['mean_years_schooling_z'] = zscore(master_df['mean_years_schooling'])\\n\\n# Create composite (higher = more inequality)\\nmaster_df['edu_ineq_index'] = -master_df['tertiary_enrollment_z'] - master_df['mean_years_schooling_z']\\n```\\n\\n## Phase 4: Handle Missing Data\\n\\n### Step 4.1: Missing Data Patterns\\n```python\\nimport seaborn as sns\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\n\\n# Heatmap of missing data\\nplt.figure(figsize=(12, 8))\\nsns.heatmap(master_df.isnull(), cbar=False, File size <300MB.\",\n  \"dataset_search_plan\": \"PHASE 1: DOWNLOAD RAW DATASETS (Parallel Execution)\\n\\n1.1 V-Dem v.14 Country-Year Dataset\\n- PRIMARY SOURCE: https://v-dem.net/static/website/data/archive/v14/V-Dem-CY+Other-CS-Coding_14.csv\\n- Size: ~200MB, contains 500+ variables for 200+ countries (1789-2024)\\n- Extract columns: country_name, year, v2x_libdem, v2pepwrsoc\\n- V-Dem Codebook v.14 defines v2pepwrsoc as 'Political equality: extent to which political power is distributed equally across social groups' (0=elite monopoly, 4=equal power)\\n- FALLBACK: If CSV download fails, use aii-hf-datasets to search 'v-dem democracy' OR download Quality of Government (QoG) Standard Dataset from http://www.qog.pol.gu.se/data/qog_basic (includes V-Dem variables)\\n\\n1.2 Identify Post-1990 Democratizers\\n- Method: Filter V-Dem data to countries where v2x_libdem transitions from <0.5 to >=0.5 during 1990-1995\\n- Expected countries (35-45): Post-communist (Poland, Hungary, Czech Rep, Slovakia, Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Slovenia, Croatia, Romania, Bulgaria, Albania, N. Macedonia, Serbia, Ukraine, Georgia, Armenia, Moldova, Kyrgyzstan, Mongolia), Latin America (Brazil, Argentina, Peru, Ecuador, Bolivia, Paraguay, El Salvador, Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua), Sub-Saharan Africa (South Africa, Ghana, Benin, Mali, Malawi, Zambia, Tanzania, Senegal, Kenya, Nigeria, Uganda), Asia (Indonesia, Philippines, Thailand, S. Korea, Taiwan)\\n- CODE: pandas read_csv with low_memory=False, filter to 1990-2024, identify democratizers\\n\\n1.3 World Bank PIP Gini Coefficients\\n- PRIMARY: World Bank Open Data API for indicator SI.POV.GINI\\n- URL: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SI.POV.GINI?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n- NOTE: Skip first row (metadata) when reading CSV\\n- FALLBACK 1: SWIID (Standardized World Income Inequality Database) - https://fsolt.org/swiid/ - download CSV from website\\n- FALLBACK 2: UNU-WIDER WIID database - https://www.wider.unu.edu/database/wiid\\n- Expected: Gini coefficients (0-100 scale) for 35-40 countries, 1990-2024\\n\\n1.4 UNESCO Education Data (Two Proxies)\\nProxy A: Tertiary Enrollment Rates\\n- SOURCE: UNESCO UIS API or data download center\\n- Indicator: SE.TER.ENRR (Gross enrollment ratio, tertiary, both sexes %)\\n- URL: http://data.uis.unesco.org/ (use query tool, export CSV)\\n- FALLBACK: Our World in Data - use aii-owid-datasets search 'tertiary education enrollment'\\n\\nProxy B: Mean Years of Schooling\\n- SOURCE 1: Barro-Lee Dataset v3.0 - http://www.barrolee.com/data/bl_v3.zip\\n  * File: bl_v3.dta (Stata) - use pandas.read_stata() to convert\\n  * Variable: total_edYrs (mean years, age 15+ or 25+)\\n  * Coverage: 146 countries, 1950-2015 (supplement with newer UNESCO data to 2024)\\n- SOURCE 2: UNDP HDR API - https://hdr.undp.org/data-center/statistical-data-api\\n- FALLBACK: OWID 'mean years of schooling' - search via aii-owid-datasets\\n\\n1.5 Education Spending (% of GDP)\\n- PRIMARY: World Bank WDI indicator SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS (Government expenditure on education, total % of GDP)\\n- URL: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\n- SECONDARY: UNESCO UIS indicator X_DC_PPP (public expenditure on education as % of GDP)\\n- TERTIARY: OECD SOCX - https://www.oecd.org/social/expenditure.htm (SOCX_2023_CSV.zip)\\n\\nPHASE 2: DATA CLEANING AND MERGING\\n\\n2.1 Country Name Standardization\\n- Problem: V-Dem, World Bank, UNESCO use different country names\\n- Solution: Use V-Dem country_name as master, create crosswalk dictionary for common variants\\n- Use ISO3 codes as merge key (more stable than names) - add using pycountry library\\n- CODE: Create dictionary mapping variants, e.g., {'United States': 'United States of America', 'Russia': 'Russian Federation'}\\n\\n2.2 Merge Strategy\\n- Start with V-Dem data filtered to post-1990 democratizers (master)\\n- Left join Gini by (country_name, year)\\n- Left join tertiary enrollment by (country_name, year)\\n- Left join mean years schooling by (country_name, year)\\n- Left join education spending by (country_name, year)\\n- CODE: pandas merge with how='left', validate='m:1'\\n\\n2.3 Create Education Inequality Index (Proxy)\\n- Since direct education inequality measures by SES unavailable, create proxy:\\n- Standardize tertiary_enrollment and mean_years_schooling (z-scores)\\n- edu_ineq_index = -tertiary_enrollment_z - mean_years_schooling_z (higher = more inequality)\\n- CODE: from scipy.stats import zscore; master_df['edu_ineq_index'] = -zscore(tertiary) - zscore(mean_years)\\n\\nPHASE 3: HANDLE MISSING DATA\\n\\n3.1 Missing Data Analysis\\n- Generate missing data heatmap (countries x years)\\n- Calculate % missing by variable and by country\\n- Identify if missing At Random (MAR) or Not At Random (MNAR)\\n\\n3.2 Imputation\\n- Option A: MICE (Multiple Imputation via Chained Equations) using sklearn IterativeImputer\\n- Option B: Listwise deletion (complete cases only) for robustness checks\\n- Recommendation: Create TWO versions - imputed and complete cases\\n- CODE: from sklearn.experimental import enable_iterative_imputer; IterativeImputer(max_iter=10, random_state=42)\\n\\nPHASE 4: EXPLORATORY DATA ANALYSIS (EDA)\\n\\n4.1 Descriptive Statistics\\n- Summary stats table (N, mean, SD, min, max, median) for all continuous variables\\n- Save to eda_descriptive_stats.csv\\n\\n4.2 Correlation Matrix\\n- Compute Pearson and Spearman correlations\\n- Test hypothesis preview: correlation of (Gini x edu_ineq_index) with v2x_libdem\\n- Save to eda_correlation_matrix.csv\\n\\n4.3 Temporal Plots\\n- Line plot: v2x_libdem trajectories by country (spaghetti plot)\\n- Line plot: Gini coefficient trends\\n- Save to eda_figures/ (PNG format)\\n\\n4.4 Data Quality Checks\\n- Validate ranges: v2x_libdem [0-1], v2pepwrsoc [0-4], Gini [20-70]\\n- Check for duplicates: assert no duplicate (country, year) pairs\\n- Check N countries: assert >=30\\n- Save to data_quality_report.txt\\n\\nPHASE 5: OUTPUT FILES\\n\\n5.1 Final Dataset (JSON)\\n- Format: JSON Lines (one JSON object per line) for efficient streaming\\n- Schema: {country, year, v2x_libdem, v2pepwrsoc, gini, tertiary_enrollment, mean_years_schooling, education_spending_gdp, edu_ineq_index, post_1990_democratizer, democratization_year}\\n- Save as data_out.json (full), data_out_mini.json (3 rows), data_out_preview.json (truncated for logging)\\n\\n5.2 Validation\\n- Use aii-json skill to validate JSON schema\\n- Use aii-file-size-limit to check file size <300MB\\n\\n5.3 Documentation\\n- Create dataset_documentation.md with: data sources/URLs, variable definitions, country list, missing data summary, EDA references\\n\\nFALLBACK PLANS:\\n- If V-Dem fails: Use QoG dataset or Polity V (https://www.systemicpeace.org/polityproject.html)\\n- If WB Gini fails: Use SWIID or UNU-WIDER WIID\\n- If UNESCO fails: Use Barro-Lee or Penn World Table 10.0\\n- If education spending fails: Use IMF GFS or impute from GDPpc\\n\\nTIME BUDGET (6 hours total):\\n- Download: 1h (parallelize with asyncio)\\n- Clean/merge: 2h\\n- Missing data: 1h\\n- EDA/validation: 1.5h\\n- Documentation: 0.5h\\n\\nUse aii-parallel-computing skill for parallel downloads and imputation.\",\n  \"target_num_datasets\": 4\n}\n",
  "old_str": null,
  "new_str": null,
  "insert_line": null,
  "view_range": null
}

first row (metadata) when reading CSV\\nFALLBACK 1: SWIID (Standardized World Income Inequality Database) - https://fsolt.org/swiid/ - download CSV from website\\nFALLBACK 2: UNU-WIDER WIID database - https://www.wider.unu.edu/database/wiid\\nExpected: Gini coefficients (0-100 scale) for 35-40 countries, 1990-2024\\n\\n1.4 UNESCO Education Data (Two Proxies)\\nProxy A: Tertiary Enrollment Rates\\nSOURCE: UNESCO UIS - Indicator SE.TER.ENRR (Gross enrollment ratio, tertiary, both sexes %)\\nURL: http://data.uis.unesco.org/ (use query tool, export CSV)\\nFALLBACK: Our World in Data - use aii-owid-datasets search 'tertiary education enrollment'\\n\\nProxy B: Mean Years of Schooling\\nSOURCE 1: Barro-Lee Dataset v3.0 - http://www.barrolee.com/data/bl_v3.zip\\nFile: bl_v3.dta (Stata) - use pandas.read_stata() to convert\\nVariable: total_edYrs (mean years, age 15+ or 25+)\\nCoverage: 146 countries, 1950-2015 (supplement with newer UNESCO data to 2024)\\nSOURCE 2: UNDP HDR API - https://hdr.undp.org/data-center/statistical-data-api\\nFALLBACK: OWID 'mean years of schooling' - search via aii-owid-datasets\\n\\n1.5 Education Spending (% of GDP)\\nPRIMARY: World Bank WDI indicator SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS (Government expenditure on education, total % of GDP)\\nURL: https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/all/indicator/SE.XPD.TOTL.GD.ZS?format=csv&per_page=10000&date=1990:2024\\nSECONDARY: UNESCO UIS indicator X_DC_PPP (public expenditure on education as % of GDP)\\nTERTIARY: OECD SOCX - https://www.oecd.org/social/expenditure.htm (SOCX_2023_CSV.zip)\\n\\nPHASE 2: DATA CLEANING AND MERGING\\n\\n2.1 Country Name Standardization\\nProblem: V-Dem, World Bank, UNESCO use different country names\\nSolution: Use V-Dem country_name as master, create crosswalk dictionary for common variants\\nUse ISO3 codes as merge key (more stable than names) - add using pycountry library\\nCODE: Create dictionary mapping variants, e.g., {'United States': 'United States of America', 'Russia': 'Russian Federation'}\\n\\n2.2 Merge Strategy\\nStart with V-Dem data filtered to post-1990 democratizers (master)\\nLeft join Gini by (country_name, year)\\nLeft join tertiary enrollment by (country_name, year)\\nLeft join mean years schooling by (country_name, year)\\nLeft join education spending by (country_name, year)\\nCODE: pandas merge with how='left', validate='m:1'\\n\\n2.3 Create Education Inequality Index (Proxy)\\nSince direct education inequality measures by SES unavailable, create proxy:\\nStandardize tertiary_enrollment and mean_years_schooling (z-scores)\\nedu_ineq_index = -tertiary_enrollment_z - mean_years_schooling_z (higher = more inequality)\\nCODE: from scipy.stats import zscore; master_df['edu_ineq_index'] = -zscore(tertiary) - zscore(mean_years)\\n\\nPHASE 3: HANDLE MISSING DATA\\n\\n3.1 Missing Data Analysis\\nGenerate missing data heatmap (countries x years)\\nCalculate % missing by variable and by country\\nIdentify if missing At Random (MAR) or Not At Random (MNAR)\\n\\n3.2 Imputation\\nOption A: MICE (Multiple Imputation via Chained Equations) using sklearn IterativeImputer\\nOption B: Listwise deletion (complete cases only) for robustness checks\\nRecommendation: Create TWO versions - imputed and complete cases\\nCODE: from sklearn.experimental import enable_iterative_imputer; IterativeImputer(max_iter=10, random_state=42)\\n\\nPHASE 4: EXPLORATORY DATA ANALYSIS (EDA)\\n\\n4.1 Descriptive Statistics\\nSummary stats table (N, mean, SD, min, max, median) for all continuous variables\\nSave to eda_descriptive_stats.csv\\n\\n4.2 Correlation Matrix\\nCompute Pearson and Spearman correlations\\nTest hypothesis preview: correlation of (Gini x edu_ineq_index) with v2x_libdem\\nSave to eda_correlation_matrix.csv\\n\\n4.3 Temporal Plots\\nLine plot: v2x_libdem trajectories by country (spaghetti plot)\\nLine plot: Gini coefficient trends\\nSave to eda_figures/ (PNG format)\\n\\n4.4 Data Quality Checks\\nValidate ranges: v2x_libdem [0-1], v2pepwrsoc [0-4], Gini [20-70]\\nCheck for duplicates: assert no duplicate (country, year) pairs\\nCheck N countries: assert >=30\\nSave to data_quality_report.txt\\n\\nPHASE 5: OUTPUT FILES\\n\\n5.1 Final Dataset (JSON)\\nFormat: JSON Lines (one JSON object per line) for efficient streaming\\nSchema: {country, year, v2x_libdem, v2pepwrsoc, gini, tertiary_enrollment, mean_years_schooling, education_spending_gdp, edu_ineq_index, post_1990_democratizer, democratization_year}\\nSave as data_out.json (full), data_out_mini.json (3 rows), data_out_preview.json (truncated for logging)\\n\\n5.2 Validation\\nUse aii-json skill to validate JSON schema\\nUse aii-file-size-limit to check file size <300MB\\n\\n5.3 Documentation\\nCreate dataset_documentation.md with: data
